Bitcoin holds $75K as AI regulation fears see KOSPI halted after 8% crash

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin consolidated as fears over US AI regulation spread to Asia, causing South Korea to announce measures to calm stock-market volatility.

Bitcoin (BTC) held above $75,000 on Friday as a sell-off in US technology stocks spread to Asian markets.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD trading near its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), at $75,733.

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50-week SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

The pair fell as much as 6.6% on Thursday as US stock markets saw sustained selling throughout the Wall Street trading session. The S&P 500 and tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite closed down 4.4% and 5.5%, respectively.

Nasdaq Composite Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

On Friday, Asian stocks extended the sell-off. South Korea’s KOSPI Composite Index fell more than 8% shortly after the open, triggering a circuit breaker that halted trading for 20 minutes. The index then pared losses to close 6% lower at 5,059.69 points, its lowest close since March 31.

KOSPI one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

At an emergency meeting, Korea’s deputy prime minister and finance minister Lee Hyoung-il announced measures to reduce forced selling.

Brokerages will be exempt from a requirement to maintain a 140% collateral ratio on margin loans for three months from Monday, local media reported. Authorities said they would prepare futher measures if volatility increased.

“We recognize with gravity the expanding volatility in global stock markets following the U.S. midterm elections,” Chosun quoted an unnamed government source as saying.

Analysts linked Thursday’s Wall Street losses to fears of tighter AI regulation after Democrats won the House and Senate. Those fears compounded existing market concerns about the sector’s capital spending.

Related: Bitcoin, Ether ETFs post $530M outflows as election gains evaporate

Citing an X post by Democratic Rep. Yassamin Ansari, who called for a “pause on advanced AI development,” market commentary service The Kobeissi Letter said an outright US ban on AI was unlikely.

“In our view, a ban of AI will not happen in the US (with near-certainty). Such a ban, if even possible to enforce, would mean the US surrendering its position in the global AI race to China. That won’t happen,” it wrote in an X post.

Kobeissi noted that Thursday’s losses were concentrated in tech, with the S&P 500 declining just about 2.5% excluding the sector. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index, which tracks 30 semiconductor companies, finished Thursday 12.2% lower.

Philadelphia Semiconductor Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Economist Mohamed El-Erian also called for common ground on AI policyt.said that lawmakers would need to find common ground on AI policy.

“America’s ‘let it rip’ approach to AI aligns with the technology’s productivity potential and national security imperatives. However, it has fallen short not only on safety and diffusion, but also on bringing society along,” he wrote in a Substack note.

El-Erian said the backlash reflected “genuine concerns,” information failures and behavioral factors.

Cointelegraph previously reported that Democrat midterm sweeps under Republican presidents have historically been followed by strong stock returns. Bitcoin’s correlation with the S&P 500, has become more positive in recent months.

Huddersfield Town are top of the table after 14 fixtures and have lost only once in the league this season. HTAFC are three points clear of Stockport County and Oxford United, with Luton Town, Plymouth Argyle and Reading then chasing them down.

The Terriers drew 1-1 at home to Luton Town last time out with in-form attacker Joe Taylor getting their goal against the Hatters. They are back in action this weekend against Exeter City in the first round of the FA Cup.

Tozer, who played for Newport County, Cheltenham Town and Wrexham, has delivered his verdict on the third tier promotion picture on The Essential EFL Podcast: “Take nothing away from Stockport, Luton, Oxford, Plymouth Argyle. Drury has also managed to get the best out of Taylor following his return from his loan at Wigan Athletic. He has found the net on 10 occasions in the league already.

The Terriers need to hope both Fernandes and Taylor can stay fit as they aim to keep their momentum going. Their upcoming cup tie against Exeter City could be an opportunity to play some fringe players and youngsters.

How to Choose the Best Crypto Advertising Network

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Do this: ask an advertising provider for a sample campaign report before you ask for a proposal.

Can you see which audiences converted? Does conversion mean a registration, a wallet connection, or a completed transaction? Can you detect where the ads appeared and spending on placements that aren’t generating results?

These questions will tell you much more than a professionally made slide showing millions of impressions. After all, you are choosing a company to spend your budget, and you need to understand what it intends to buy, how it measures it, and what it changes.

Here is what you should be checking before you commit.

When a provider says it reaches “crypto users,” that’s not enough. Ask what qualifies someone for this label. Is it someone who once read a Bitcoin article, or an active trader who is comparing exchanges right now? Each of these suggests a very different audience.

Here is what a good answer sounds like. Ask Blockchain-Ads, the platform Coinbase, Binance and OKX buy on, and you get a segment with a definition and a recency window: active crypto traders, people who recently compared exchanges or wallets, or your own depositors as a retargeting group. That precision is what a demand-side platform is for, and Blockchain-Ads was the first one built for crypto, iGaming and finance. Hold every network on your shortlist to the same standard.

For example, if you are promoting a lending protocol, your question should be whether you can reach a segment of people interested in DeFi lending, and how recently they showed that interest. Then you should request an audience estimate for your target countries.

When you compare crypto advertising networks, a comparison of crypto ad networks gives you a starting shortlist. Then get specific:

A publisher list will tell you where your ads might appear. It won’t necessarily tell you who will see them.

There are two different buying approaches to understand here:

Let’s say you sell a wallet for active DeFi users. A crypto news placement could reach experienced users alongside casual readers. Audience targeting could reach relevant users while they browse other subjects.

Now here is the trick. Neither approach automatically wins. Your task here is to ask the provider what they support and whether you can apply contextual restrictions when you need them.

The most important test for an advertising network that serves crypto exchanges is whether it can connect the advertising spend to funded accounts and trading activity.

For a wallet, the event could be a completed swap.

For a blockchain game, it could be a player returning after a first session.

Give the provider your event definition and ask the following:

The best crypto advertising network for your project needs to measure the actual outcome.

Tip: A public transaction doesn’t automatically reveal which ad acquired the user. Blockchain-Ads’ attribution guide explains that campaign identifiers must be linked to account or wallet activity.

Start with the conversion definition. Then, examine the headline number.

Ask for evidence that matches your situation: similar product, target market, budget, and conversion event. Then check the following:

Minimum deposits, daily budgets, and service eligibility are separate commitments when you pick a crypto advertising network.

Blockchain-Ads, for example, publishes its minimum deposit and daily budget, and offers a self-serve platform or a managed option run by industry experts.

Ask any provider for the same clarity. You need to be able to confirm management fees, creative costs, top-up requirements, and the treatment of unused funds.

Finally, ask what the proposed budget can reasonably test. You need a clear explanation of how the funds will be split across countries, formats, and audiences.

A total CPA can hide expensive placements behind cheaper ones.

Ask for a demonstration of reporting by audience, creative, country, device, and placement. The idea here is to confirm which breakdowns are available together and which require support or an export request.

Try a very practical question during the demo:

“If mobile conversions drop next week, what can I do to investigate this?”

Ideally, you should be able to examine whether the delivery shifted toward a different audience or country.

Tip: Ask how quickly the reports update, and check whether existing customers can be excluded from acquisition campaigns. Otherwise, a campaign may receive credit for people you already acquired. Remember: the best crypto ads in a presentation are less useful than reporting that helps you decide what deserves your money.

Fraud protection needs much more explanation than a badge on a well-designed website.

Ask what gets filtered before buying, what gets checked afterward, and how you can report suspicious activity. Find out whether you can pause delivery during an investigation and what evidence the provider needs.

Blockchain-Ads, for instance, documents independent impression verification, traffic monitoring, allow and block lists, and frequency controls. It also asks advertisers reporting problems to supply the campaign ID and period.

Use these categories when you talk to providers:

Tip: Clarify the privacy requirements, too: what customer data you can upload, how it is protected, how deletion requests work, etc.

Finally, ask who will review performance and what would trigger a change.

Marko Rendevski, SVP Advertising Services & Partnerships at Blockchain-Ads, describes the principle clearly: “We don’t make decisions based only on CTR or a small number of clicks.”

Have the provider explain how they will assess your campaign against your target conversion cost and normal conversion delay. Someone who deposits several days after registration needs time to appear in the results.

If you are wondering how to choose a crypto ad network, finish it all with this one question:

“What will we know after this first test?”

Choosing a crypto advertising network is not simple. Before you commit, ask the provider to show you how it would build your audience, track your conversions, and report where your money went. Get the costs and the test plan in writing. You should leave that conversation only once you know what the first campaign will test and how you can judge the results.

Strive (ASST) Posts $474.8 Million Common Net Income as Bitcoin Yield Hits 63.2%

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Strive reported $474.8 million of net income attributable to common stockholders for the third quarter ended Sept. 30, a print that arrived Friday alongside a company claim that Class A total return had outperformed bitcoin by 103.7% year to date through the same date.

GAAP net income was $509.0 million, or $5.24 a share on a fully diluted basis. Almost all of that came from a $533.7 million unrealized gain on digital assets marked to fair value. Operating revenue for the quarter was just $2.7 million, so the earnings line is a treasury mark, not a fee business.

In a post on X that led with the outperformance figure, Strive also highlighted a year-to-date bitcoin yield of 63.2% and a bitcoin dollar gain of $9.00 per assumed diluted share outstanding, both measured through Sept. 30. Chairman and CEO Matthew Cole tied those metrics to the stock’s relative showing against the asset.

“Strive has continued to successfully execute on its treasury management strategy, delivering a strong bitcoin yield year to date in 2026, which I believe is the primary driver of our common equity’s outperformance relative to Bitcoin so far this year,” Cole said in the earnings release.

Bitcoin yield, as Strive defines it, is the percentage change in bitcoin per share over a period — not a cash yield. On that measure the company posted 18.5% in the third quarter alone and 19.3% from Oct. 1 through Oct. 30, after the SATS preferred debut.

Strive acquired 8,136 bitcoin in the third quarter at an average cost of about $78,885 and 20,373 bitcoin over the nine months ended Sept. 30. Another 15,124 coins followed between Oct. 1 and Oct. 30. Holdings stood at 28,000 bitcoin on Sept. 30 and 43,124 by Oct. 30, keeping Strive among the largest public corporate stacks after recent multi-thousand-coin buys and smaller weekly adds that still trailed Strategy.

Digital assets at fair value were $2.34 billion on the Sept. 30 balance sheet. Cash had risen to $490.4 million by Oct. 30, and Strive still held Strategy’s variable-rate STRC preferred with a fair value of about $50.1 million.

The capital stack that funded the buys is mostly perpetual preferreds. On Oct. 20 Strive closed a registered offering of 7 million shares of its 15% Series A perpetual preferred (SATS) for about $609 million in net proceeds. Preferred dividends of $34.2 million in the quarter are what turned the $509.0 million GAAP profit into the $474.8 million common figure.

Strip the story to nine-month GAAP and the picture flips. For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Strive posted a net loss attributable to common stockholders of $88.4 million. The third-quarter profit is real under fair-value accounting, but it is concentrated in one mark-to-market line.

ASST itself has not moved in a straight line with the company’s bitcoin-yield scoreboard. Shares closed at $20.59 on Thursday, down about 10% on the day, and traded near $20.70 early Friday. That leaves the stock roughly 29% below its level a year earlier, when Yahoo Finance data put the close near $29, and about 33% under its Oct. 21 local high of $30.75.

The 52-week range on Yahoo Finance runs from $7.02 to $34.40. Year to date, the price path from the Jan. 2 close near $17.30 into the mid-$20s is a gain of roughly 20%, with a February low near the 52-week floor and a September push back toward $30. Through Sept. 30 — the cutoff on Strive’s outperformance claim — ASST had risen about 70% from that January close while bitcoin was down roughly 7% over the same window on Yahoo Finance data, a wide gap even before the company’s own total-return methodology.

The longer chart still carries the scar of 2025, when Asset Entities became the public shell for Strive’s bitcoin-treasury reverse merger and the share price spiked into triple digits on weekly closes before collapsing. Investors who held through that conversion saw a different ride than the tidy year-to-date relative-return figure Strive put at the top of Friday’s note.

Cole said the recent SATS issuance leaves Strive “never been in a better position to generate long-term returns in excess of Bitcoin for our common equity shareholders.” The test for ASST from here is whether another quarter of bitcoin yield and preferred-funded buys can reopen the gap to bitcoin after a year in which the stock still sits well below where it traded twelve months ago, even as the treasury has climbed past 30,000 then 40,000 bitcoin.

BlackRock Sells $105 Million of Bitcoin as Recovery Stalls Again

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

BlackRock, one of the world's leading asset management firms, has extended its prolonged Bitcoin selling spree, having continued to dump the asset since late October.

While BlackRock has yet to record any new Bitcoin purchase this month, the latest market data shows that it has sold Bitcoin again following the severe price downturn seen in the last day.

Latest data showcased on SosoValue shows that BlackRock's Bitcoin ETF has recorded another net outflow for the fourth time this week, as it continues to post negative daily trading sessions throughout the week.

Amid consistent withdrawals from investors, BlackRock has sold $104.97 million worth of Bitcoin after IBIT recorded another massive outflow on November 5th.

Although Bitcoin initially showed signs of a potential recovery earlier this week, the market has since reversed and the asset is back to trading in the red.

While the massive Bitcoin sale from BlackRock has arrived at a time when the market is facing another

volatility, it appears that institutional investors are rapidly withdrawing their funds and exercising caution to hedge against further losses.

Bitcoin has just witnessed its sharpest price reversal of the month, falling from a high of over $80,000 to a low of $75,114 within just 24 hours.

Following this sharp price drop, Bitcoin has seen a price decline of 5.22% over the past day, and it is now trading at $75,979 as of the time of writing.

Although the market has remained volatile for several weeks, the significant sell-off seen in the last day has sparked further concerns among both retail and institutional investors as expectations for a November price recovery begin to fade.

FBI agent drains crypto wallets tied to 35 investigations, steals $2.47 million and plans Portugal escape

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The former counterintelligence agent used sensitive wallet credentials to execute roughly 100 fraudulent transfers.

A former FBI agent stole $2.47 million in crypto using classified investigative records, compromising federal investigations into criminal organizations and foreign adversaries.

The US Department of Justice disclosed on Nov. 5 that Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, pleaded guilty to wire fraud and money laundering after exploiting his access to crypto wallet credentials collected during FBI investigations.

His scheme involved about 100 unauthorized transfers using credentials tied to 35 separate FBI investigations, including wallets belonging to suspected criminals, adversarial nation-state actors, and at least one wallet the bureau created for an operation.

Prosecutors said the thefts compromised multiple investigations involving criminal groups and foreign adversaries, along with sensitive intelligence-gathering methods.

The disclosure exposes a significant internal security failure at the FBI, where an agent entrusted with protecting national security turned information gathered through federal investigations into personal enrichment.

According to prosecutors, Yaroch worked in the FBI's Boston Division from 2017 until early 2025, investigating threats involving a foreign adversary.

He subsequently joined the bureau's Counterintelligence and Espionage Division, where he had access to systems containing cryptocurrency credentials obtained through investigations.

The thefts began no later than December 2024, before his transfer to the division.

An FBI affidavit filed in August revealed that Yaroch searched bureau records for crypto wallets associated with foreign adversaries, memorized their recovery credentials, and transferred funds into a personal wallet.

Two routes intersected: access to wallet secrets in FBI records enabled transfers from investigation-linked wallets.

Investigative access to wallet credentials enabled personal transfers, including from at least one FBI operational wallet.

The affidavit's personal-wallet account predates the broader plea scope. The total proceeds and transfer count describe the entire scheme, not a single wallet balance. The diagram does not identify affected cases, all destinations, security controls or a network breach.

Schematic maps the credential-access account in the Aug. 1 affidavit onto the broader wallet categories and approximate scheme totals in DOJ's Nov. 5 plea announcement. Connector sizes are not quantitative. $2.47M is the rounded reported proceeds total; no new arithmetic.

The affidavit's personal-wallet account predates the broader plea scope. The total proceeds and transfer count describe the entire scheme, not a single wallet balance. The diagram does not identify affected cases, all destinations, security controls or a network breach.

He initially told investigators that frustration with the FBI's inability to disrupt certain adversarial crypto accounts had led him to take matters into his own hands.

However, the misconduct expanded into a broader theft and money laundering scheme.

After taking the crypto, Yaroch converted portions into other digital assets and moved funds through additional transactions to conceal their origins.

Between November 2025 and January 2026, he transferred approximately $93,082 into an account and used money moved between his accounts to cover credit card bills, vehicle payments, and other debts.

The Justice Department said his actions interfered with the FBI's ability to protect public safety and national security while compromising sources and methods used in criminal investigations and foreign intelligence collection.

Prosecutors did not identify the affected countries or investigations, leaving the full extent of the intelligence damage undisclosed.

Beyond paying personal debts, Yaroch was preparing to use the proceeds to establish a new life in Europe, according to federal investigators.

Court documents revealed that the former agent turned to ChatGPT for advice on investing approximately $1 million and relocating to the European Union.

After discussing his interest in vineyard ownership and a slower lifestyle, the AI chatbot recommended Portugal as a potential destination.

Yaroch subsequently took concrete steps toward establishing himself in the country.

On June 15, he granted power of attorney to Portuguese lawyers to register him with the country's tax authorities and obtain a tax identification number.

Investigators also discovered plans for a September trip to Portugal. The August affidavit disclosed that Yaroch had previously traveled abroad without reporting some of those trips to the FBI, despite security requirements.

His plans unraveled in July when he voluntarily disclosed his cryptocurrency thefts to Justice Department personnel.

The initial investigation identified roughly $1 million in suspected stolen cryptocurrency. However, the subsequent criminal case found that the scheme generated about $2.47 million.

The FBI terminated his employment on July 31, and investigators recovered approximately $925,426 in cryptocurrency from accounts he controlled that day. An additional $165,582 remained in dollars at Kraken, according to the affidavit.

Yaroch is scheduled to be sentenced on March 4, 2027, and faces up to 20 years in prison.

Palmer has played almost every minute of Chelsea’s Premier League campaign this term with his injury problems from last season seemingly behind him.

"It's a brave new world": GTA 6's PC port will release sooner than you think to avoid losing money to AI rip-offs, says former Rockstar tech director

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"If it's GTA, I think people are gonna try it."

GTA 6's PC release may come sooner than anticipated, according to former Rockstar North technical director Obbe Vermeij, because Rockstar and publishers Take-Two will be concerned about losing money to unofficial generative AI clones of the PS5 version, once it launches on 19th November. In general, Vermeij thinks that how to avoid having your games duplicated by chatbot herders is "the big question" right now for devs and publishers, particularly those who don't have the means to enforce their intellectual property dominions as vehemently as Take-Two do for Grand Theft Auto.

Speaking to me during a wide-ranging chatathon, Vermeij reflected on the early days of Grand Theft Auto development at Rockstar North in the 90s, and how the Noel-and-Liam-Gallagher-esque relationship between PC and console gaming has evolved. He shared a sweary anecdote about the company's difficulties getting the original GTA 1 to look the business on Sony's PlayStation.

"I didn't work on GTA 1 and 2, but I worked for the company at the time, and I was actually right next to the GTA 1 team, so I was able to follow it," he said. "So they worked on PC and then an external company in Dundee did the PlayStation version. Which was really weird, because like, PCs were so much more powerful - still the case, but in those days, it was even more so.

"And I remember, at the time, the GTA team was definitely the most rowdy of all of the teams," Vermeij continued. "And there was one particular day where they sent the PC build away, and then it came back from the PlayStation people, and they had cut down all the textures from 256 colours to 16 colours, and my god, the artist was just going around and swearing - 'what the fuck did they do to our textures, I can't believe it, we can't release this shite'. So it always has been that sort of awkward relationship between PC and the console. So in those days, it was like PC first and then console second, even though that didn't really make much sense for the sales, because the PlayStation versions sold way better."

When it came time to develop GTA 3, Rockstar "flipped that around", Vermeij went on, creating the console versions first. "Clearly the PC market has sort of grown, caught up with the console market, but there's still that same mindset," he said. Most publishers prefer to release all versions at once, Vermeij added, "because then there's only one marketing campaign they have to pay for." GTA lives by its own rules, however. "I guess it's less of an argument for them because the game is just so big, so they just kind of always left it that way. And for developers, it's way easier to just do one version at a time. So, you know, that makes sense."

The arrival of generative AI threatens to throw all this to the winds. Tools such as Reverse Engineer Anything allow for speedy unofficial ports or "slopcomps" of console games on PC, despite warnings from seasoned recomp developers that using genAI for this purpose wrecks the community aspect of unofficial porting, and results in illegible "bruteforce" code rather than preserving some understanding of how the original game was made.

"It really has changed the world," Vermeij observed, "and I think I would be concerned, if I was Rockstar, that somebody would just take the PS5 version and get AI to change it to PC, which won't be won't be as good as their effort, but it could still also hurt their sales. So, I wouldn't be surprised if there was an effort to get [the releases] a little bit closer together. Obviously, I don't really know what they're thinking."

GTA 6 is, according to Rockstar and Take-Two at least, an enormously fiddly production, costing hundreds of millions of dollars over eight years in development. Nonetheless, Vermeij thinks current genAI tools are "probably" capable of pulling off a port. "It will take a long time. It actually, physically takes hours and hours and hours to do these things. And it will take a bunch of tokens and everything, so it would probably cost like, tens of thousands of dollars in tokens. But like, you know, if it's GTA, I think people are gonna try it."

There are already signs that genAI PC port goldrushers are ready to pounce on GTA 6. Just today, Ars Technica reported on efforts to yassify the game's PS5 trailer footage by piping it through a stronker PC graphics card and using Nvidia's DLSS 5 to apply extra layers of gloss.

Vermeij himself left Rockstar way back in 2009 and is now an indie developer. His current early access project is Plentiful, a hexagonal god sim with an emphasis on water physics. It uses "AI-assisted machine translation for localization", but not for "gameplay content, art, or audio". Vermeij is, in general, interested in generative AI despite the surging tide of slopcomp ports. Smaller indie developers are, of course, more vulnerable to the financial fallout from having their work unofficially cloned by generative AI, but Vermeij thinks it's an existential worry for megacorps like Take-Two as well.

"I don't really see how you could protect against that, to be honest," he told me in closing. "I don't see it. But a big company like Take-Two, they would be able to get the lawyers on the case, and maybe, like, you said, for a big game, maybe the AI doesn't really do a particularly good job. But yeah, I mean, it's a brave new world. What was the case before, is that there's so much effort in developing the game, that it is not possible to rip it off, and that's completely changed now. So, yeah, we're going to have to see in the next couple of years, how that's going to pan out."

The latest rumour-mongering places GTA 6's PC release sometime in early 2027. In other news, Rockstar staff are soon to formally vote on whether to apply for union recognition. If the organisers carry the day, the resulting union could encompass nearly all the company's UK staff below management level.

Former Wolves, Sheffield United and Leicester City man retires aged 33 after ‘tough’ recent years

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Conor Coady was let go by Wrexham in August, with the club and player agreeing a mutual termination of his contract. The former Wolves captain has been without a club since, although there were links with a move to Swansea City shortly before his departure from the Racecourse Ground.

Now though, aged 33, Coady has announced his decision to retire from professional football. The defender confirmed the news on his Instagram, with a video thanking his former clubs, coaches, teammates, family and friends for their support along the way.

Coady hangs up his boots after over 500 club career appearances and 10 England caps. He played 317 times for Wolverhampton Wanderers and also spent time with Sheffield United, Huddersfield Town, Leicester City, Everton, Charlton Athletic, Wrexham and Liverpool.

In a video message shared on his Instagram, Coady bid a fond farewell to the game. He said:

“Football is my life. It always has been, but everything at some point comes to an end. I never ever thought that playing for Wolves in Europe or going to with England a World Cup this day would come. But honestly, the last couple of years have been tough – not playing as much as I would have liked I have struggled with, as I have lived for playing footy.

“Coming through at my boyhood club and putting the red shirt on was one of the proudest days, as no one could ever take that away from me. Sheffield United and Huddersfield were the perfect clubs for me and made me grow, then came Wolves, the place I needed and the place that made me a man.

“Eight of the best years, with the best people, in a city that made me their own. My kids were born in Old Gold, and you made Amie (Coady’s wife) truly enjoy football. For that, it will always be home.”

Coady would go on to highlight his pride at having played for Everton at Goodison Park, before reflecting on tougher times at Leicester City and Wrexham. He said:

“On to Leicester, then Wrexham, which were tough at times but filled with amazing people and amazing supporters who care deeply about their club. The Charlton, who put a smile back on my face.

“The truth is, I’m ready now. I’ve given football everything and I will continue to do so in my next chapter, but it’s time for my incredible wife Amie and my four boys to enjoy seeing Daddy that little bit more.”

After personal thanks to his close family, friends, supporters and long-serving agent James Lippett, Coady signed off by saying: “It’s been an honour to say I played this unbelievable game. Thank you for everything. Coads.”

Coady has cut a popular figure among fans and teammates alike across his playing career. It means there was no shortage of support for the retiring defender upon his announcement.

Wolves players of past and present sent their best wishes, including Kieran Trippier, Raul Jimenez, Romain Saiss, Daniel Podence and many more. Ex-Everton teammates Jack Grealish and James Tarkowski congratulated him too, alongside other prominent figures like former England boss Gareth Southgate, retired boxer Tony Bellew and broadcasters Kelly Somers and Suzi Perry.

Coady has worked in punditry in recent years, so it will be intriguing to see if he ventures further into this world after his retirement. If a future in coaching awaits, his personable character and leadership abilities would make him an excellent addition to a club’s academy or first-team staff.

Here’s How Major Galleries’ UK Operations Performed in 2025

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The good news: The United Kingdom is still the world’s second-largest art market by value, according to the latest Art Basel & UBS Art Market Report. The not-so-good news? Since 2009, UK sales have grown just 14 percent, while the United States market has grown 51 percent.

The art market seesaw keeps tilting. This fall, many galleries with UK branches filed their annual reports with Companies House, the UK government agency that keeps records for businesses registered in the country. Some, like David Zwirner, Lisson, and Sprüth Magers, saw their turnover (the UK term for revenue) grow, while others, like Hauser & Wirth, Sadie Coles HQ, and Pace, saw it drop. There was no one-size-fits-all story for the UK market in 2025.

ARTnews pulled the filings for 10 of these galleries to get a sense of how the trade is doing.

Pace’s 2025 report showed that its international business posted a $5.6 million loss in 2025. The figures cover The Pace Gallery Limited, the London-registered company that operates the gallery’s London space and owns its subsidiaries in Hong Kong, Beijing, Seoul, Geneva, and Berlin. They do not include Pace’s US or Tokyo operations.

Turnover fell 2.6 percent, from $67.7 million in 2024 to $65.9 million in 2025. Meanwhile, cost of sales rose 12 percent, gross profit dropped $7.4 million to $12.8 million, and year-end cash on hand fell from $34.8 million in 2023 to $24.1 million in 2024 and $11.1 million in 2025.

CEO Marc Glimcher told ARTnews that the gallery is “all off the bottom” and said it had a record performance at Art Basel Paris in October, though he did not offer specifics.

“We’ve done a lot of internal reengineering of the way the companies work together. The successes we’ve had in Berlin and Japan have been very, very instructive, and we have had an extremely profitable year already in Los Angeles,” Glimcher said. “The things we’ve done are already bearing fruit, and they’re bearing fruit because the market has substantially changed.”

Lisson’s 2025 financial year ended on January 31, 2026. In the filing for its parent company, Art Holding Co. Limited, the group reported a 9 percent increase in turnover, to $130 million (£97 million) from $119.42 million (£89 million) in 2024.

The group described the period as a “year of encouragement despite the wider art market continuing to face many macro-economic driven challenges.” The gallery attributed its recovery to “a couple of challenging years coupled with a renewed focus on stock and secondary sales activities.” Lisson added in the filing that the risks and uncertainties facing the business continue to relate to “inherent volatility in the contemporary art market,” and that it mitigates that risk “as far as possible by offering work from a diverse spread of artists across a wide price spectrum to a broad range of private and institutional collectors, both in the UK and internationally.”

A spokesperson for Lisson told ARTnews that while the audit shows a decrease in profit for both Lisson Gallery London Limited and Art Holding Co. Limited, Lisson’s global gross profit has increased six percent year-on-year and the losses were largely due to non-trading foreign exchange movements.

“Lisson has been able to maintain this and recover efficiently due to a number of factors, including a renewed focus on secondary sales activities and securing distribution fees (despite significant upward inflationary pressures on freight costs),” said the spokesperson.

Hauser & Wirth’s turnover fell 13.1 percent, from $90.82 million (£68.29 million) in 2024 to $78.93 million (£59.35 million) in 2025. However, the company remained profitable in 2025, with profit up 51 percent year on year, totalling £403,318 profit for 2025 against 2024’s £266,539. In the filing, the company said its directors “have a strong belief in the future of the company and are pleased to report continuing profitability during a period of art market fluctuations.”

In the filing, the gallery also noted that the UK “represented a small element of the annual program in 2025,” with nine exhibitions and two art fair presentations there, compared with 58 exhibitions and 13 fair presentations worldwide. At Frieze London and Frieze Masters in 2025, the gallery sold just under $21 million worth of works.

“As a global business, the UK company’s results are not indicative of the gallery’s overall performance, which includes larger markets such as Switzerland and the US,” a Hauser & Wirth spokesperson told ARTnews. “The gallery has a strong commitment to the UK, reflected in the major project underway to create a new London flagship on South Audley Street in Mayfair. 2026 has been a year of significant growth and investment for the gallery globally, including new spaces in Palo Alto, the Hauser & Wirth Publishers HQ in Zurich and Stockwell House the community-led space in Somerset as well as five new artists and estates joining the roster.”

At Goodman Gallery, which has locations in Johannesburg, Cape Town, New York, and London, turnover for the UK business rose 7 percent in 2025 to $47.3 million (£35.26 million), while pre-tax profit soared 60.8 percent to $6.37 million (£4.75 million). Cost of sales fell 2.3 percent to $31.95 million (£23.82 million).

“We anticipate healthier financial performance in the 2026/2027 financial year,” Liza Essers, owner and director of Goodman Gallery, told ARTnews.

The gallery is celebrating its 60th year in business, and the anniversary has helped drive sales, according to Essers. She added that the gallery’s most recent William Kentridge exhibition, which opened in September in London, has “surpassed expectations,” with films selling for $485,000 and works on paper priced from $250,000 to $850,000. Essers noted that the gallery had a strong showing at Art Basel Paris, including several significant sales to long-term clients from Europe, North America, and Asia. This year the gallery also launched Goodman Gallery Advisory, a secondary-market arm focused on 20th-century artists ranging from Alexander Calder to Yayoi Kusama.

Pilar Corrias saw its turnover fall 42.7 percent, from $25.76 million (£19.2 million) in 2024 to $14.75 million (£10.99 million) in 2025.

In the filing, the gallery said its main challenges were supply chain issues, market volatility, digital security, logistics such as shipping, the recoverability of debts, and foreign currency fluctuations. Looking ahead, the gallery said that for 2026 and beyond it would focus on reaching more collectors worldwide by showing at major international art fairs, investing in digital infrastructure to grow its online presence and improve security, and strengthening its relationships with private collectors, institutions, and museums.

The gallery did not respond to a request for comment.

Monika Sprüth & Philomene Magers Limited, the holding company of Sprüth Magers gallery, returned sharply to profitability in 2025, reporting a pre-tax profit of $1.83 million (£1.36 million) after a $2.34 million (£1.75 million) pre-tax loss in 2024. Turnover rose 66.2 percent to $24.77 million (£18.46 million).

The accounts also broke down turnover by region. UK turnover more than doubled, to $139,047 (£103,643) from $63,938 (£47,689) in 2024. Turnover from Europe rose 74.1 percent to $17.31 million (£12.9 million), while turnover from outside Europe rose 49.3 percent to $7.32 million (£5.45 million).

“Based on the performance in 2025 and current trading, the directors consider the company to be in a strong financial position as it enters 2026,” the filing said. “The outlook for 2026 is positive and the directors expect a similar level of performance to 2025, while recognising that results remain sensitive to market conditions and the timing of high-value transactions.”

The gallery did not respond to a request for comment.

Sadie Coles HQ’s turnover fell 10 percent in 2025 to $34.63 million (£25.81 million), but the gallery’s pre-tax profit rose 57.2 percent, from $536,279 (£399,731) in 2024 to $843,063 (£628,401).

“Careful curation of the gallery programme and the addition of new represented artists means that the company is continuing to attract new collectors and to expand its business,” the gallery said in the filing. It noted that turnover was slightly lower than in 2024, despite a significant increase in the total number of sales.

In fall 2025, Sadie Coles HQ opened a new space on Savile Row, which the gallery said has helped it “attract new artists of calibre.” The gallery added that it “is confident of continued success.”

Timothy Taylor reported turnover of $23 million (£17.21 million) in 2025, up 3.7 percent from $22.26 million (£16.59 million) in 2024. Cost of sales rose 5.9 percent to $17.4 million (£12.96 million), while gross profit fell 2.5 percent to $5.68 million (£4.24 million). In the filing, the gallery said it “intends to continue strengthening its brand presence in the UK, the US and Asia.”

The gallery did not respond to a request for comment.

2025 marked Thaddaeus Ropac’s ninth year of trading in the UK, and the gallery’s founder recently told ARTnews that he had renewed its lease on Dover Street for another 10 years. The gallery’s 2025 turnover fell 4.9 percent to $46.58 million (£34.72 million), while pre-tax profit fell 7.3 percent to $3.32 million (£2.47 million).

In the filing, the gallery said that 2025, like 2024, reflected “uncertainties around economy and sociopolitical upheavals,” but that it remained “profitable in this climate, a reflection of its strong engagement with its customers and artist and close control over costs.”

The gallery’s UK staff grew from 23 in 2024 to 25 in 2025. That year, Thaddaeus Ropac also opened a space in Milan, adding to its locations in London, Paris, Salzburg, and Seoul.

According to David Zwirner’s 2025 filing with Companies House, the gallery’s turnover rose 56 percent, from $49.72 million (£37.13 million) in 2024 to $77.51 million (£57.89 million) in 2025. The gallery attributed the $27.79 million (£20.8 million) jump in sales revenue to “increased art fair sales as well as revenue recognition timing.”

At the 2025 edition of Frieze London, the gallery reported sales led by a Chris Ofili painting for $700,000. It also sold a painting by Steven Shearer for $550,000, two paintings by Lucas Arruda priced from $320,000 to $350,000 each, a work on paper by Elizabeth Peyton for $300,000, a work on paper by Lisa Yuskavage for $180,000, and two works by Wolfgang Tillmans priced from $115,000 to $250,000. The gallery also sold works by Kerry James Marshall, Christopher Williams, Huma Bhabha, Sosa Joseph, and Frank Walter for undisclosed five-figure sums.

The gallery did not respond to a request for comment.

LeBron James Shares the Gym Moves That Keep Him Unstoppable on the Court at 41

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

LeBron James has just become the first player in NBA history to reach 16,000 field goals and at 41 is proving that age is just a number when it comes to his recent performances with the Philadelphia 76ers. There’s no doubt that this basketball icon puts in the work, and a recent glimpse into his workouts reveals that mobility and reaction work is keeping him sharp.

“LeBron James putting in the work,” read the caption on a recent post shared by the NBA’s official Instagram account, alongside a video that provided the blueprint for any aspiring basketball player. The video was posted less than a week before James’ regular season debut for the Sixers and showed the work that this dedicated athlete still puts in behind the scenes, in order to dominate on the court.

The workout showed that James gets to grips with the kettlebell to stretch his obliques and strengthen his core, while also combining movements for mobility. For example, James picks up a pair of dumbbells and starts out with a shoulder press, but then brings the dumbbells together at the top, and slowly lowers them for a downward elbow squeeze. This combination builds LeBron’s upper body with added time under tension, with the arm rotation being a great way to work on mobility. He even adds a rear-delt fly at the end of the rep to further tax his shoulders and chest.

Of course, LeBron is never too far away from a basketball, and in the gym, the four-time NBA champion works on his reaction times by having a partner throw the ball to his side, while kneeling on a bench. This drill removes “King James’” lower body from the equation and forces him to balance on the bench while turning to the side and catching the ball with one hand, then returning it right back in one swift movement. Studies show that when athletes work on reaction times away from their chosen sport, their game benefits from improved cognitive performance. And, we already know that this drill has translated to further points on the court. For his lower body, James sits into dumbbell weighted squats and undertakes further drills to build his explosive power and stopping abilities.

With the Philadelphia Sixers currently leading the Western Conference, and LeBron James now in his record-extending 24th season, the basketball icon appears to be just as hungry as ever. “I didn’t leave my family (in Los Angeles) to lose in the second round. I know that for sure,” commented the player before this season began. “Work needs to be done and I’m ready to get to work.”

Transformers Company Announces New Indie Publishing Label Focused On Toy-Like Games

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Toy company Takara Tomy, creator of the likes of Beyblade and Transformers, has this week announced an all-new indie game publishing label, the fifth such brand to be announced in the last year in a shake-up of Japanese gaming. Takara Tomy revealed the Enjoyn Games initiative alongside a trio of toy-themed titles, the first two of which will be released later this month.

According to Takara Tomy, Enjoyn Games aims to bring the companyâs experience making toys into guiding the development of new games. In that sense, its initial lineup has the feeling of more toy-like experiences, each emphasizing multiplayer. The first game, Stack and Sus, is a three-to-six-player social deduction game, with players divided into staff and spies, trying to make or sabotage a burger. Then there’s Sloppy Forgeries, a competitive party painting game tasking players with recreating classic art but with no ability to undo actions and all in a limited time, where accuracy determines a winner. Both of these games will release next week, on November 10 and 14 respectively. The third game, likely due in December, is Little Cheese Works, a puzzle-platformer for up to four players using voice chat to coordinate as mice negotiating levels to grab cheese, all without disturbing dastardly cats.

The crossover between gaming and toys for Takara Tomy seems obvious: each of these multiplayer experiences feels like a board game the company could have released, and there’s the potential to lean on the broader toy infrastructure in the company for promotion, and for future expansion to other mediums. It also, notably, makes Tomy the latest in a slew of major Japanese companies establishing transmedia initiatives that includes branching into gaming. Just before Tokyo Game Show, Sanrio announced Sanrio Games, with plans to release 10 games featuring Sanrio characters over the next three years. Toei Games has been set up to take the movie giantâs experience into indie publishing, also with a potential to broaden the IP if it’s successful. Then there’s PARCO Games, a division of the major Japanese department store Parco, which officially launched its label at the end of last summer.

Even beyond major studios, new publishers are embracing Japanese gamesâlast month Visions of Mana director Kenji Ozawa moved his Studio Sasanqua into the indie publishing space with its first game, Afantasia. This is on top of more established Japanese publishers like Playism and Shueisha Games, and the launch of a publishing label by Palworld developer Pocketpair. In almost all of these cases, the desire is not just to make money selling games, but to cultivate ideas that can be established in other mediums. The potential to find a game that could become something larger, as seen by The Exit 8, has been a driving goal for many. Whatever the motivations, Takara Tomyâs new game publishing label is a great endorsement of the Japanese indie gaming scene, as more companies and players see potential and excitement in the works of the country’s solo devs and smaller teams.

SCI-Arc task force champions long-term architectural strategies for fire-prone areas

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

the sci-arc resilient futures task force supports los angeles homeowners recovering from the 2025 wildfires, offering climate-resilient architectural solutions to replace outdated building habits.

The SCI-Arc Resilient Futures Task Force was created to rethink standard rebuilding methods after the January 2025 fires in Los Angeles exposed deep weaknesses in Southern California’s default residential. Bringing together teachers, researchers, and students, the initiative gives displaced homeowners direct, practical help with building safety, soil toxicity, emergency funding, and insurance claims through public community meetings.

Nearly two years later, residents are still rebuilding their lives as wildfires become more frequent and severe. Instead of repeating past mistakes caused by outdated safety codes, standard construction habits, and insurance gaps, this crisis presents a critical moment to create safer, long-term architectural designs in fire-prone areas.

Grounded in the institution’s tradition of critical design research, the Resilient Futures Task Force connects academic expertise directly with real-world disaster recovery. The coalition brings together an international network of architects, structural engineers, scientists, material specialists, non-profit developers, and financial strategists. Key collaborators and participating institutions include Code Red Consultants, Angel City Lumber, SGH, JPMorgan Chase, Century Housing, Clearinghouse CDFI, Builders FirstSource, Plant Prefab, Sexsmith Architects, Timberlab, ReSAWN, Urban Machine, and TU Delft.

Extending its focus from temporary aid to permanent resilience, the Task Force organized public panels on alternative building materials, mass timber construction, rebuilding economics, and fire-adapted landscaping to help communities make informed, long-term architectural decisions.

The initiative’s first year concluded with Architecture After the Fires, a landmark public exhibition showcasing nearly 70 speculative and actionable home design proposals tailored for fire zones. As the largest collection of Wildland-Urban Interface (WUI) architectural proposals to date, the showcase featured ongoing work from prominent practices including Lorcan O’Herlihy, SPF:architects, Studio Murnane, Morphosis, Heather Flood and Story Dimson Studio, Knowhow Shop, and Scott Uriu.

The Task Force is publishing a comprehensive book documenting these projects, creating a permanent reference guide for building in high-risk environments. Next, SCI-Arc plans to expand its research through institutional partnerships that combine artificial intelligence, environmental risk modeling, and advanced simulation to shape resilient building standards across the region.

school: SCI-Arc | @sciarc location: Los Angeles, California

Qantas Launches Historic New 15-Hour Nonstop Route From Sydney: See Map Now

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

In an unexpected and highly intriguing network development, Qantas has announced a brand-new long-haul route. The oneworld member will begin flying from Sydney to the increasingly popular Cape Town.

It'll be historical in various ways. For example, it'll become Qantas' record third route to South Africa, while being the first time that Australia has had flights to Cape Town. It'll also be Cape Town's new longest route within the Southern Hemisphere. However, it will be far from Qantas' longest nonstop service on the Boeing 787.

On October 19, 2027, during the spring in the Southern Hemisphere, Qantas will lift off from its busiest and critical hub of Sydney Kingsford Smith Airport (SYD) to Cape Town International Airport (CPT). On a great circle basis, the brand-new route will cover 5,945 nautical miles (11,010 km) each way.

As this represents the shortest distance between two airports, real-world operations will increase that distance. There won't be any payload restriction, which means a full load of passengers and belly freight.

QF89: 10:15 AM-3:55 PM (same-day arrival; 14 hours and 40 minutes)

QF90: 5:55 PM-2:50 PM+1 (12 hours and 55 minutes)

Like so many new routes, SYD-CPT will be flown three times weekly. Given the block time and stage length of the airport pair, this means that one Boeing 787-9 will be able to operate it.

All of Qantas' 787-9s have 236 seats, which is not many. That's mainly because they're quite premium, with 42 seats in business (1-2-1), 28 in premium economy (2-3-2; 38" pitch), and 166 in economy (3-3-3; 32"). Around 18% of the capacity is in business.

Booking data shows that 87,000 round-trip passengers flew between Australia and CPT in 2025, with traffic up by nearly a fifth compared to 2024. While many South Africans live in Australia, it is unclear how many come from the CPT area. Many of the passengers would have been Australians visiting the increasingly popular city for tourism.

Unsurprisingly, SYD was the largest market to/from CPT. With around 23,000 passengers, the local traffic was not overly high, although the new nonstop flights may grow that by around 40%. The airline will also target passengers connecting to Auckland Airport (AKL), Brisbane, and Melbourne, in particular, with those places having around 42,000 passengers.

Significantly, SYD-CPT is relatively high-yielding, with the average fare across all airlines, cabins, and passengers being US$1,335 one way (excluding taxes but including a fuel surcharge). Reflecting higher premium demand, the average fare for SYD-CPT is around 12% higher than for SYD to Johannesburg's O. R. Tambo International Airport (JNB).

Interestingly, SYD-CPT flights will cover marginally less distance than SYD-JNB. As such, the fare difference is particularly notable, especially when coupled with similar operating expenses. Commenting on the new route, senior airline network planner, African specialist, and industry commentator Behramjee Ghadially said that:

"This new service will perform very well for Qantas, seeing how the Aussies love to visit new exotic places, especially where a combination of beach, wine and safari can be experienced in an upscale manner [although most safari-related activities are in eastern South Africa].

Qantas will also target tour operators selling South Africa packages from Australia and New Zealand as they can now offer an open-jaw itinerary for their clientele, such as flying into JNB and flying back from CPT using the same airline."

The carrier has just grown its footprint to this continent considerably. Find out more here!

As the above map shows, Qantas will now fly from SYD to CPT and JNB, along with from Perth International Airport (PER) to JNB. The latter route is the only one on which the airline has head-to-head competition, as it competes directly with the troubled South African Airways. It is also the one on which Qantas deploys an Airbus twinjet.

OAG data shows that Qantas flew between PER and JNB until 2023, with the route, which is nearly entirely overwater, returning 11 months ago, in December 2025. The operating A330-200 flies AKL-PER-JNB-PER-AKL. At some point, it then operates to SYD, where the specific airframe is switched.

Jackie Tohn Knows Getting Eliminated From <i><em>American Idol</em></i> Was a Gift

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The actress, who stars in Nobody Wants This, answers the “First, Now, Next” questionnaire

For the better part of four decades, Jackie Tohn has been steadily chipping away at Hollywood, building a career that has taken her from New York stages to the big and small screens. She has a particular kind of onscreen magnetism. As Esther, the scene-stealing sister-in-law to Adam Brody’s Rabbi Noah and Kristen Bell’s Joanne Williams in Nobody Wants This, she brings a particular kind of onscreen magnetism. Even at her most withering, she radiates the kind of energy that makes you want to be her best friend.

The New York native got her start onstage before landing early television roles in shows including The Nanny, Angel, and Veronica Mars. In 2017, a standout role in Netflix’s Emmy-winning series GLOW cemented her place in the industry. And did we mention she competed on Season 8 of American Idol? Out of about 150,000 hopefuls, she made it to the top 36. (The girl can sing.)

This year, Tohn has made headlines for reasons beyond her acting, opening up about learning she carries a BRCA1 gene mutation, which led her to undergo a preventative double mastectomy and, soon after, take up advocacy work. She’s been refreshingly candid about the experience. (Really, just ask her.) And she’s approached it the way she seems to approach everything in life: with grace, with wit, and with a laugh, because sometimes that’s just how you gotta roll.

A few weeks before the third season of Nobody Wants This dropped, Tohn sat down with Harper’s Bazaar to chat about sisterhood (and spitting in test tubes), an encounter with her idol, Joan Rivers (who fell asleep during Tohn’s performance in Jewtopia), and her Hollywood origin story (which includes Jessica Biel). Read on for her answers to our “First, Now, Next” questionnaire.

Cracked something open in me? There are so many that come to mind. My favorite movies in my early 20s were the ones that really cracked something open. Like Election and Rushmore and Wet Hot American Summer. Oh! And Waking Life, which was this movie that was rotoscoped, where they filmed it and drew over it. Those were all movies where I was figuring out who I was, and each of those movies really influenced me in a lot of ways.

My first true friend was Lisa Molina. Our mothers were very good friends. We met going on auditions because we were both theater kids, singing. My first celebrity friend was Ben Salisbury, and then my first celebrity best friend was Jessica Biel, who I lived with and is the reason I moved to L.A.

I came out to California in between semesters because we had this two-month break. My agent at the time, Aggie Gold, of the Fresh Faces Agency out of Baldwin, Long Island, famously said, “It’s pilot season, let’s see what we can get.” So me and my agent and my mom came out to L.A., and I was Ben Salisbury’s date to the TV Guide Awards in 1998, and I met Jessie Biel and never left. I’m 18 years old, and because my mom was with me, she got to meet Jessie’s parents, so she didn’t feel like she was releasing me to the wolves of living alone in Los Angeles. I lived in a gated community in Calabasas with Jess and her family. Kim Biel taught me how to write a check.

Honestly, I don’t know if there are better ones. Frankly, at the TV Guide Awards, the tables had polyester table skirts, and it was at a Marriott. It was delightful.

I’d been acting since I was nine, and I hadn’t really gotten the thing that was going to hit. When I was 23, I finally got a series. They fired everyone from the pilot, then it was picked up for six episodes mid-season, and I booked the series.

On our first work week, my parents came out, because it was a live studio audience. The next day, I was going to work and the producers called. They wanted me to come to their cabana at CBS Radford. My parents were in the car, so I go to drop them off at my dressing room, and someone was painting my name off my parking space. I was like, “Oh! Maybe they’re moving me.” I didn’t drop my parents off because I had no dressing room anymore; the door was locked. I drove them over to the cabana, and I was like, “What is going on?” And the first words out of their mouths were “Jackie, please calm down.” I’m like, “No, I’m not doing anything. My parents are in the car. This is mortifying.” That’s when they told me that the network wanted to go another way. And that the girl who was in the pilot, who I replaced, was now re-replacing me. I got fired. I made six episodes of network money. I had enough money at 23 to sort of be a person.

I got more into theater and more into music at that point because I was like, I can’t be at the behest of this business. I can’t be sitting here waiting for the phone to ring. I was playing a lot more music and doing a lot more theater. And that totally changed my life, because after this series I did a play called Jewtopia and moved to New York, and we did it off-Broadway for a few years. That’s when I started making records and really touring the country.

The second failure was getting eliminated from American Idol. That felt devastating, but it was the biggest gift. I didn’t make it to the top 24, and that’s where they would have really owned me.

Well, you never think it’s all going to be fine. As a person who started acting when she was nine and started getting real jobs at 36, those 27 years don’t go away when you finally get the thing you want. That said, the first time I had that feeling that I was part of something special, that mattered, was definitely the TV show GLOW. I remember being cognizant of it while it was happening. Me and Betty Gilpin would sit next to each other and be, like, “Just so you know, this is never going to happen to any of us ever again. This thing that we are doing right now and this sisterhood that we created and this female-powered camaraderie on this show, that’s tackling women’s issues, that’s written by, starring, directed by women? Executive produced, showrun, everything. This is a lightning-in-a-bottle situation.” You felt it when you were there.

I define career success as being financially stable in the career you want to have. There’s financial stability, but people are miserable. They have a lot of money, but they hate their job. For me, it was having enough money to get by in the arts and not have an additional 5–7 p.m. closet-cleaning, organizing job. When I was 16, it was to be a TV star. To be [Fran Fine in] The Nanny, specifically. The eye on the prize was, have that. Star in a half-hour multicam where I come down the stairs in various short sequined dresses.

Then, of course, the barometer by which I measure life success and personal success is so different. It’s having love in my life with my partner and my dogs, and having my health, having gone through what I have in this last year. Getting my genetic testing and doing whatever preventative surgeries I have to do.

The sisterhood is wild. I partnered with this genetic-testing company, Myriad. They discovered the BRCA mutation. I try to tell as many people as I can: Spit in a tube. It’s so easy.

I am my mother, so the answer to that is everything. The way we look, our facial expressions, the way we speak. I was that really rogue teenager who was always obsessed with my mom. I was hyper-aware that she was funny and smart and cool, and all the kids wanted to come to our house because my parents were the cool, lenient ones who always had the snack foods. I didn’t have a curfew because my mom was like, “Everyone else has a curfew. What are you going to do, stay out alone? You’ll come home as late as the last person comes home.”

My dad always played the piano, and I always sang with him. I could look back and be like, “Was that embarrassing?” No. I was ready; I was a showman at four. That was maybe a little annoying, when my mother would always want me to sing. But it wasn’t even that annoying. I wanted to.

My boyfriend and I were just in France and Italy over the summer. Both of our first times.

When I tell you it was grim until 36, now you believe. It’s crazy. Now that I’ve been, it’s even more unhinged. I bought Gucci bags in Florence, and they are proprietary to Gucci in Florence. They don’t go anywhere; they just stay there. I bought one for myself and one for my mom.

This is sort of a vague answer, but in general, I have trouble spending money on something I already have. If I’m out and I’m using a camera that has film, but I have three rolls of film in the fridge, it makes me insane to buy film because I have film. It used to be flying well, but it’s not anymore. Now that’s the number-one place it goes. Even hotels I don’t splurge on. When I’m doing comedy or speaking engagements around the country, I’m happy with a Hilton. I don’t need to be at a Ritz-Carlton for one night in the middle of Atlanta when I’m there for 18 hours. I’m this weird combination of … I wouldn’t say frugal, but I’m very mindful of how I spend my money. I am deeply not wasteful.

We are nothing alike! I’m just kidding. I know the biggest parallel and the biggest difference. The biggest parallel is that we are both absolutely strong, capable, opinionated Jewish women. I think something that differentiates us … I am a very, very foolish fool. It’s my whole thing. I love silly and whimsy. The sillier the better, the goofier the better. Talking in stupid voices. I’ve just been truly a clown my entire life, and Esther is not that way. She’s very straitlaced; she wants to be taken seriously. As I get older, it’s a little more important for me to feel like I’m being taken seriously, both as an actor and as a woman with opinions. But Esther’s not goofy like me. Esther would never be eating chips right now.

I’m saying yes to sisterhood. And no to people watching videos or FaceTiming on their phone in public without headphones.

Maybe I’d live on a ranch with a hundred dogs who need homes and need specialty care and are my very, very close friends who are dogs. I love dogs so much, I could fucking explode. It’s hard for me to walk down the street when I see dogs. Literally, I’m like, “How come that one’s not talking to me? How come that one is only with their own mom, and I get turned into a crazy person??

"More than an absence of colour": 4 artists reveal the keys to working in black and white

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Explore projects, trends and inspiring design work.

Discover design software, tools and creative resources.

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more.

Packed with digital art tutorials, inspiration and expert advice from ImagineFX.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The creative possibilities are endless if you know how to approach it.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

With a whole spectrum of colours only a few clicks away, perhaps it’s not surprising that working in black and white is sometimes overlooked.

But whether you’re working monochromatically to draft an illustration or using it for a finished piece, a black and white approach is full of benefits. It can even make you stand out in an art world that's saturated with vibrant talent (if you need the tools for your own art, see our guides to the best drawing tablets and the best pens for artists).

Foregoing colour can be an intentional creative choice rather than the result of a limitation, but for illustrator Sarah Koudelka-Mason it was born out of convenience. “After trying out all sorts of tools, I found pencils to be the simplest, least fussy medium,” she says. “All you need is a pencil and paper, which you can bring anywhere. Perhaps because pencils were also my first and most consistent tool in my art journey, I feel the most comfortable with them.”

For character artist and illustrator Joe Vriens, a black and white approach was the result of a fear of colour theory mixed with a dash of impatience.

“I’d always get excited to see the bare line work of a comic, too; it seemed much more achievable,” he reveals. “I could grab a pen and paper and make a comic without expensive markers, paints or digital tools.

“I’m also old enough to remember King Kong in black and white. I made my parents rent that thing probably once a month. In retrospect, maybe King Kong informed my approach all along?”

Illustrator Isabella Mazzanti points out that working in black and white plays into a long tradition of art styles, including her own influences which range from Gustave Doré’s engravings to contemporary manga.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

“They seemed accessible and reproducible to me,” she says. “These early experiences were fundamental, because they shaped my aesthetic sensibility and my approach to drawing.”

Seeing as working in black in white is a deliberate approach, it also depends on specific considerations.

“You have to make sure the shading values are balanced well,” Sarah advises. “It’s easy for your shading to look flat or ’muddy’ if your tones are too similar, so you really have to figure out your darks, mediums and lights, and then tie them together with the between shading. I personally like to work lighter and work my way to the crisp dark lines at the end. So satisfying.”

“Line weight and shape design are also key,” says concept artist Alexander Joseba, who has worked for Epic Games, HBO, Ubisoft and more. “A strong silhouette can communicate a character or object very quickly, and different line weights can help separate forms and create depth. Since you cannot use colour to separate materials or planes, you need to do it through values, textures, edges and rhythm.”

“For me, black and white forces you to be more disciplined, although I also like to play with a more wild and instinctive approach. If the image works in black and white, it usually means that the composition, lighting and structure are solid.”

Alexander grew up looking at the work of great artists such as John Buscema, Mike Mignola, Alberto Breccia and many others.

“I was fascinated by the way they were able to communicate so much with ink: how they separated planes, created depth, controlled light and shadow, and made the whole image work so clearly, each one with their own very distinct style,” he explains

Some extra considerations are required when working in black and white, however. Alexander believes the most important consideration is readability.

“Without colour, you have to rely much more on values, contrast, shapes, silhouettes and composition to guide the viewer’s eye. Everything needs to be clear from the beginning: what the focal point is, what belongs to the foreground, middle ground and background, and where the main light is coming from.

“Working in black and white forces you to focus on the foundation of the image. You need to solve composition, lighting, values, silhouette, volume and overall readability properly. I think any artist who works well in black and white will also improve their colour work, because they understand values, light and structure better. Colour does not replace that foundation; it complements it.”

Isabella also warns against assuming that working in black and white is a simpler approach.

“A solid grasp of the visual language is essential in order to use it effectively,” she argues. “Black and white is not simply the absence of colour; when working with chiaroscuro, one chooses to tell a story based on duality, contrast and the exaggeration of ambiguity. I believe it is vitally important for an artist to understand this element, which has the power to intensify the viewer’s emotional engagement.”

When it comes to making black and white art work professionally, Isabella stresses that economic forces may be influencing publishers just as much as aesthetic considerations. “It lends images a classic, refined and timeless quality,” she says.

“Even when telling a certain type of story – for example, Gothic literature – black and white seems to be the most natural choice. It can be a risky choice, because one is foregoing the emotional appeal of colour, but when used in the right way and in the right context, it’s a winning decision.”

Sarah claims that black and white art is underrated, and that it’s not the usual approach commissioners are looking for. “But at the same time, if there are clients specifically looking for works of that nature and you’ve established yourself in that niche, you will find them. This applies to any unique niche of art, and I personally enjoy the challenge of going against the grain!”

For Alexander, black and white artwork is useful for both commercial production pipelines and for final illustration styles. “It depends on the project: sometimes it is a practical development tool, and sometimes it becomes the final artistic language,” he says.

Even if artists don’t gear their black and white artwork towards clients, focusing on it can still improve their overall craft. “Balancing tones in a final image is super-important, so if you can master greys then moving on to colour will feel easier,” says Joe.

“You can guide the viewer’s eye with tones alone, so it can help your compositions greatly. Focusing on greys or even black and white can take a lot of the pressure off as well, which allows you to really dig into lighting and form.”

Meanwhile Sarah is a firm believer that practising black and white art is one of the best ways to strengthen your understanding of the fundamentals.

“Without the distraction or support of vibrant colours, you are encouraged to look at your work in a simpler and more intentional way,” she says. “You start to see shape language, values, lighting, contrast, composition and depth a lot clearer.

Isabella agrees: “The absence of colour changes the way an image is perceived,” she says. “Rather than depriving the image of something, a black and white approach reveals its essential and most intimate nature; it speaks to the viewer in a more universal, almost archetypal language.”

Gigantic chasm on Mars is still tectonically active

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →
